\Hypertarget{dma-mapping_8h_a3c11476e4dc8e04e2c6ea3a66bc83207}\index{dma-\/mapping.h@{dma-\/mapping.h}!DMA\_ATTR\_MMIO@{DMA\_ATTR\_MMIO}}
\index{DMA\_ATTR\_MMIO@{DMA\_ATTR\_MMIO}!dma-\/mapping.h@{dma-\/mapping.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{DMA\_ATTR\_MMIO}{DMA\_ATTR\_MMIO}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{dma-mapping_8h_a3c11476e4dc8e04e2c6ea3a66bc83207} 
\+\#define DMA\+\_\+\+ATTR\+\_\+\+MMIO~(1UL $<$$<$ 10)}

DMA\+\_\+\+ATTR\+\_\+\+MMIO -\/ Indicates memory-\/mapped I/\+O (MMIO) region for DMA mapping

This attribute indicates the physical address is not normal system memory. It may not be used with kmap\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}()/\+phys\+\_\+to\+\_\+virt()/\+phys\+\_\+to\+\_\+page() functions, it may not be cacheable, and access using CPU load/\+store instructions may not be allowed.

Usually this will be used to describe MMIO addresses, or other non-\/cacheable register addresses. When DMA mapping this sort of address we call the operation Peer to Peer as a one device is DMA\textquotesingle{}ing to another device. For PCI devices the p2pdma APIs must be used to determine if DMA\+\_\+\+ATTR\+\_\+\+MMIO is appropriate.

For architectures that require cache flushing for DMA coherence DMA\+\_\+\+ATTR\+\_\+\+MMIO will not perform any cache flushing. The address provided must never be mapped cacheable into the CPU. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{dma-mapping_8h_source_l00080}{80}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{dma-mapping_8h_source}{dma-\/mapping.\+h}}.

DMA\+\_\+\+ATTR\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+KERNEL\+\_\+\+MAPPING:\+ Lets the platform to avoid creating a kernel virtual mapping for the allocated buffer.

\Hypertarget{dma-mapping_8h_a725903508d725d9b43e5be85704d0c1c}\index{dma-\/mapping.h@{dma-\/mapping.h}!DMA\_MAPPING\_ERROR@{DMA\_MAPPING\_ERROR}}
\index{DMA\_MAPPING\_ERROR@{DMA\_MAPPING\_ERROR}!dma-\/mapping.h@{dma-\/mapping.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{DMA\_MAPPING\_ERROR}{DMA\_MAPPING\_ERROR}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{dma-mapping_8h_a725903508d725d9b43e5be85704d0c1c} 
\+\#define DMA\+\_\+\+MAPPING\+\_\+\+ERROR~(\texorpdfstring{$\sim$}{\string~}(dma\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t)0)}

A dma\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t can hold any valid DMA or bus address for the platform. It can be given to a device to use as a DMA source or target. It is specific to a given device and there may be a translation between the CPU physical address space and the bus address space.

DMA\+\_\+\+MAPPING\+\_\+\+ERROR is the magic error code if a mapping failed. It should not be used directly in drivers, but checked for using \doxylink{dma-mapping_8h_a7c1ad27f1b06a34281045c1cd8e76abc}{dma\+\_\+mapping\+\_\+error()} instead. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{dma-mapping_8h_source_l00106}{106}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{dma-mapping_8h_source}{dma-\/mapping.\+h}}.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{dma\_addressing\_limited()}{dma\_addressing\_limited()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{dma-mapping_8h_a51714210d4c72cc29aa3d3d266a1c5fe} 
bool dma\+\_\+addressing\+\_\+limited (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct device \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{dev}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

dma\+\_\+addressing\+\_\+limited -\/ return if the device is addressing limited @dev:\+ device to check

Return true if the devices DMA mask is too small to address all memory in the system, else false. Lack of addressing bits is the prime reason for bounce buffering, but might not be the only one.

Function Description:\+ Public API that checks if a device has limited addressing. It calls the internal function and logs a debug message if addressing is limited. This is used to determine if bounce buffering might be needed.

Function Description:\+ Allocates memory for DMA operations with attributes. It handles allocations from coherent memory pools, direct mapping, or IOMMU. It returns the CPU address and sets the DMA handle. This is the main allocation function for DMA memory. DMA allocations can never be turned back into a page pointer, so requesting compound pages doesn\textquotesingle{}t make sense (and can\textquotesingle{}t even be supported at all by various backends).

let the implementation decide on the zone to allocate from:\+

Function Description:\+ Checks if a given physical address and size are suitable for coherent DMA. It verifies that the memory region is within the device\textquotesingle{}s DMA capabilities. Function Description:\+ Checks if a device needs DMA synchronization operations. It returns true if the device requires sync operations or if debugging is enabled. This helps determine whether to perform sync operations. Always call DMA sync operations when debugging is enabled

void dma\+\_\+free\+\_\+attrs (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct device \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{dev}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{cpu\+\_\+addr}{, }\item[{dma\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{dma\+\_\+handle}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{attrs}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Frees DMA memory that was allocated with \doxylink{mapping_8c_a236209ce15e5cafa011c634bd0bb6be2}{dma\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+attrs()}. It handles releases from coherent memory pools, direct mapping, or IOMMU. It also checks for the correct context and prevents freeing in interrupt context. On non-\/coherent platforms which implement DMA-\/coherent buffers via non-\/cacheable remaps, ops-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}free() may call \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_af115207536ac97045f67feba98e766d5}{vunmap()}. Thus getting this far in IRQ context is a) at risk of a BUG\+\_\+\+ON() or trying to sleep on some machines, and b) an indication that the driver is probably misusing the coherent API anyway.

u64 dma\+\_\+get\+\_\+required\+\_\+mask (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct device \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{dev}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Returns the DMA mask that is required to address all memory in the system. This is used to determine the minimum DMA mask needed for the device to access all system memory. It returns a 32-\/bit mask by default for IOMMU users. We require every DMA ops implementation to at least support a 32-\/bit DMA mask (and use bounce buffering if that isn\textquotesingle{}t supported in hardware). As the direct mapping code has its own routine to actually report an optimal mask we default to 32-\/bit here as that is the right thing for most IOMMUs, and at least not actively harmful in general.

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{mapping_8c_source_l00681}{681}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{mapping_8c_source}{mapping.\+c}}.

!\+CONFIG\+\_\+\+DMA\+\_\+\+NEED\+\_\+\+SYNC The whole \doxylink{dma-mapping_8h_a0e02f80de68289857d25ef23c85dafc8}{dma\+\_\+get\+\_\+sgtable()} idea is fundamentally unsafe -\/ it seems that the intention is to allow exporting memory allocated via the coherent DMA APIs through the dma\+\_\+buf API, which only accepts a scattertable. This presents a couple of problems:\+
\begin{DoxyEnumerate}
\item Not all memory allocated via the coherent DMA APIs is backed by a struct page
\item Passing coherent DMA memory into the streaming APIs is not allowed as we will try to flush the memory through a different alias to that actually being used (and the flushes are redundant.)
\end{DoxyEnumerate}

Function Description:\+ Creates a scatterlist table from a DMA allocation. It converts a coherent DMA allocation into a scatterlist that can be used with the DMA API. This is useful for exporting memory through the dma\+\_\+buf interface.
